3.2.2.
Tree preservation theorem.

Theorem 3.2.2.1
.

Let
n\geq 1
and
T\in\mathcal{D}^{T}_{n}(m)
a degree
n
tree diagram on
m
strands. Then

(3.23)

Z(R(T))=1+T+O(n+1),

where
R(T)=\sigma(C_{T})
is the result of surgery along a tree clasper
C_{T}
realizing
T
(see Theorem
3.1.4.1
), and the notation
O(n+1)
means that the equality holds modulo terms of degree
\geq n+1
. In particular, the Kontsevich integral induces an inverse to
R
, which then provides an isomorphism of graded Lie
\mathbb{Q}
-algebras
\mathcal{L}^{\FI}(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}\cong\mathcal{L}L(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
. Moreover, at each step, we first make the simplifying assumption (
A
):
m=n+1
,
T
has one leg on each strand and (if
n>1
) the legs attached to the rightmost two strands are adjacent to a common node. Then we show the result for the general case.

For
n=1
and assuming (
A
), there is only one possible diagram
T\in\mathcal{D}_{1}^{T}(2)
with a leg on each strand. Its realization is a full positive braiding between the two strands, hence we obtain the desired result by Theorem
3.2.1.1
(
(iii)
):

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/degree1-realization/D.eps}}}\overset{R}{\longmapsto}\sigma\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/degree1-realization/CD.eps}}}\right)=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/degree1-realization/gamma_D+.eps}}}\overset{Z}{\longmapsto}

\displaystyle\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/base-case-TPT/empty-diagram.eps}}}+\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/base-case-TPT/chord-on-x.eps}}}+O(2)\right)\circ\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/base-case-TPT/empty-diagram.eps}}}+\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/base-case-TPT/chord-on-x.eps}}}+O(2)\right)

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/base-case-TPT/empty-straight.eps}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/base-case-TPT/chord-on-straight.eps}}}+O(2).

Now we remove the assumption (
A
), hence the single chord of
T
can have its legs attached anywhere on the
m
strands. Consider a clasper
C_{T}
realizing
T
, as in the construction of the realizing map
R
, see Figure
11
. By an isotopy, we can pull the clasper out and bring it into a small box, such that the box contains
2
parallel vertical strands and the clasper
C_{T}
is attached to them satisfying (

):

(3.24)

\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/diagram-T.eps}}}\;\rightsquigarrow\;\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/CT.eps}}}\simeq\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/pulled-out.eps}}}.

The grey box consists of a string link
\xi
with a degree
1
tree clasper

on it, satisfying (

). Thus we compute

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle\left(R\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/diagram-T.eps}}}\right)\right)=Z\left(\sigma\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/xi-out.eps}}}\right)\right)\circ Z\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/tangle-box.eps}}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\textbf{A})}{=}\left(Z\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/box-straight.eps}}}\right)+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/box-chord.eps}}}+O(2)\right)\circ Z\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/tangle-box.eps}}}\right)

\displaystyle=Z\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/box-straight.eps}}}\right)\circ Z\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/tangle-box.eps}}}\right)+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/box-chord.eps}}}\circ\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/tangle-box-empty.eps}}}+O(2)

\displaystyle=Z\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/trivial-tangle.eps}}}\right)+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/full-chord.eps}}}+O(2)=1+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/pulling-claspers-out/diagram-T-no-label.eps}}}+O(2)

where the first equality uses Figure
3.24
and multiplicativity. Then the left factor can be computed by the above since (
A
) is satisfied in the small grey box. Then we distribute the product over the sum on the left to obtain the second line, where everything that has degree
\geq 2
is contained in
O(2)
. Use multiplicativity again to reconstruct the first term as the Kontsevich integral of a string link isotopic to the unlink, which becomes
1
, while the second term gives the diagram
T
when attached to the outside. This concludes the proof of the case

.

Now we show the induction step. Let
n\geq 2
and assume that the statement holds in degree
n-1
.

First, consider a degree
n
tree diagram
T\in\mathcal{D}^{T}_{n}(n+1)
on
n+1

). It looks like the diagram on the left below, where the black dashed rectangle contains the
n-1
other legs. Realize it as a tree clasper

, assuming that the additional half-twist is in the blue blob.

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/tree_T.eps}}}\>\rightsquigarrow\;\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/clasper_C_T.eps}}}.

The core of the induction step is to apply Habiro’s move 2 and 10

to the visible tripod:

\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.3}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/move-2.eps}}}\;\overset{H10}{\sim}\;\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.3}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/move-10.eps}}}\;\simeq\>\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.3}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/combinatorial-form.eps}}}.

The right-hand-side is obtained by isotopy. The new tree clasper
C^{\prime}
(
C_{T}
minus the tripod) realizes a degree

.

Thus, to compute
R(T)
we can first apply moves
2
and

as above, then perform surgery on
C^{\prime}
. This surgery is entirely contained in the grey rectangle shown in the first line below, and we obtain that first equality by multiplicativity of
Z
:

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle(R(T))=Z\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/clasper-in-box.eps}}}\right)\circ Z\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/tangle-without-box.eps}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1+(R_{n+1})_{*}(D_{n})_{*}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/Tprime.eps}}}\right)+O(n)\right)\circ\left(1+Z_{1}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/tangle-without-box.eps}}}\right)+O(2)\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left((R_{n+1})_{*}(D_{n})_{*}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/Tprime.eps}}}\right)\right)\circ Z_{1}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/tangle-without-box.eps}}}\right)}_{(1)}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/box-empty.eps}}}\circ Z\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/tangle-without-box.eps}}}\right)}_{(2)}+\underbrace{Z_{\geq 1}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/clasper-in-box.eps}}}\right)\circ\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/empty-without-box.eps}}}}_{(3)}\;+\;O(n+1).

From the first to the second line, the induction hypothesis
Z(R(T^{\prime}))=1+T^{\prime}+O(n)
is used, with additional strand doubling and reversing. Then the third line is obtained by distributing this product
35
35

35

Observe that the
1
’s are not all the same, they stand for the empty diagram on the adequate tangle skeleton.
, and putting all the terms of degree
\geq n+1
inside
O(n+1)
. The resulting three terms are called (1), (2) and (3). Let us compute them:

(1)

The first term is a product of degree
(n-1)
terms with degree
1
terms. First, we compute those two factors individually. Since we are only computing the degree
1
of
Z
, only the braidings matter. The first factor is obtained by doubling the
n
-th strand and reversing the new
(n+1)
-st strand

\displaystyle(R_{n+1})_{*}(D_{n})_{*}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/Tprime.eps}}}\right)=(R_{n+1})_{*}

\displaystyle\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/lift-Tpr-left.eps}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/lift-Tpr-right.eps}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/lift-Tpr-left-reversed.eps}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/lift-Tpr-right-reversed.eps}}}

and the second factor is a sum of Kontsevich integrals of braidings

\displaystyle Z_{1}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/tangle-without-box.eps}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/Z1-top.eps}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/Z1-bottom.eps}}}+\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/Z1-top-right.eps}}}-\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/Z1-bottom-right.eps}}}.

Combining them yields

\displaystyle(1)

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/tl.eps}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/bl.eps}}}+\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/trl.eps}}}-\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/brl.eps}}}

\displaystyle-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/tr.eps}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/br.eps}}}-\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/trr.eps}}}+\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/brr.eps}}}

\displaystyle=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/term1.eps}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/term2.eps}}}\overset{\STU}{=}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/tree_T.eps}}}

where the last three columns vanish, since the leg attached to the blob is free to slide across the maximum. In the first column, the additional chord prevents that leg from sliding. This last column gives the tree diagram
T
by
\STU
.

(2)

This part is easier. Indeed, the left factor is
Z
applied to a trivial string link (but whose rightmost strand is reversed). By mutliplicativity of
Z
, we can combine the two factors into

Z\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/tangle_without-clasper.eps}}}\right)=Z\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/straight-tangle.eps}}}\right)=1.

(3)

Since terms (1) and (2) equal
T
and
1
, respectively, we expect (3) to vanish. Although the first factor might be very complicated, all we know is that it is obtained from a sum of diagrams by doubling the last strand, then reversing the new last strand. This fact alone actually suffices to show that (3) vanishes.

Indeed, suppose that
D
is any nonempty diagram on
n
strands, with
k\geq 1
legs attached to the last strand. Doubling the last strand turns that diagram into a sum of all its
2^{k}
lifts. Such a lift is indexed by a word
e_{1}\dots e_{k}\in\{0,1\}^{k}
where a
e_{i}=0
, resp.
e_{i}=1
, means that the
i
-th leg is attached to the
n
-th, resp.
n+1
-st strand. Reversing the last strand has the effect of mutliplying by
\pm 1
, depending on the number of legs attached to the last strand. The resulting sum

\displaystyle(R_{n+1})_{*}(D_{n})_{*}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/diagram-D.eps}}}\right)=\sum_{e_{2},\dots,e_{k}\in\{0,1\}}(-1)^{\sum_{j\geq 2}e_{j}}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/D-lift-left.eps}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/D-lift-right.eps}}}\right)

is a sum of differences of the form ”
e_{1}=0
” minus ”
e_{1}=1
”. Multiplying each such difference with the second factor of (3) gives
0
since the first leg is now free to go from one position to the other:

\displaystyle\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/D-lift-left.eps}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale={1.2}]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/D-lift-right.eps}}}\right)\circ\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[]{Ressources/Kontsevich_integral/induction-TPT/empty-without-box.eps}}}=0.

The above shows that all terms in
Z(R(T^{\prime}))
that have at least one leg on the last strand vanish once we apply
(R_{n+1})_{*}(D_{n})_{*}
and multiply the result with the second factor.

In fact, all the terms in
Z(R(T^{\prime}))
must have at least 1 leg on the last strand. Indeed, by definition of the Kontsevich integral, the sum of all terms with no leg on the last strand is equal to the Kontsevich integral of
R(T^{\prime})
minus the last strand. If the last strand is removed, then
T^{\prime}
gets a trivial leaf and the surgery is trivial. In other words, this comes from the fact that a string link obtained by surgery along a tree clasper with exactly one leg on each strand is a
Brunnian string link

[
HM06
]
.

This concludes the proof that (3) vanishes.

By the above computations of (1,2,3), we obtain

Z(R(T))=1+T+O(n+1)

as desired. This concludes the case (
A
) in degree
n
.

The general case is shown exactly as in degree
1
, by pulling the clasper out to extract a box containing the clasper (as in (
3.24
)), in which assumption (
A
) is satisfied. This concludes the induction step, and the proof of (
3.23
).

It remains to show that
Z
induces an inverse to
R
.

By (
3.23
), the degree
n
part of
Z
induces a map
Z_{n}\colon L_{n}(m)\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\FI}_{n}(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}
, which is constant on
C_{n+1}
-equivalence classes. Thus it factors through

Z^{GH}_{n}\colon\mathcal{L}_{n}L(m)\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\FI}_{n}(m)_{\mathbb{Q}},

whose image lies in
\Prim(\mathcal{A}^{\FI}(m)_{\mathbb{Q}})_{n}
. Identifying the latter space with
\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\FI}(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}
by Theorem
2.4.5.1
, direct summing all the degrees at once and tensoring with
\mathbb{Q}
, we finally obtain

Z^{GH}\colon\mathcal{L}L(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}^{\FI}_{n}(m)_{\mathbb{Q}},

which is an inverse to
R
by (
3.23
).
∎

4.
Application to the rational Goussarov–Habiro conjecture

4.1.
The rational Goussarov–Habiro conjecture

4.1.1.
Vassiliev filtration and associated graded algebra.

The monoid ring
36
36

36

If
M
is a monoid and
R
a ring, the
monoid ring

RM
is the abelian group of formal sums
\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{i}x_{i}
, where
r_{i}\in R
,
x_{i}\in M
, wih multiplication given by
rx\cdot r^{\prime}x^{\prime}=rr^{\prime}xx^{\prime}
and extended linearly.

\mathbb{Z}L(m)
of string links is filtered by the
Vassiliev filtration
(
4.1
), with associated graded algebra
\mathcal{A}L(m)
(
4.2
):

(4.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}L(m)

\displaystyle\supset\del_{1}(\mathbb{Z}S_{1}L(m))\supset\del_{2}(\mathbb{Z}S_{2}L(m))\supset\dots,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}L(m)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{A}_{n}L(m)\coloneqq\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\frac{\del_{n}(\mathbb{Z}S_{n}L(m))}{\del_{n+1}(\mathbb{Z}S_{n+1}L(m))},

where
S_{\bullet}L(m)
denotes the graded monoid of (isotopy classes of) singular
37
37

37

Singularities are only allowed to be transversal double points, and isotopies must preserve the double points.
string links, graded by the number of double points. The
\mathbb{Z}
-linear maps
\del_{n}\colon\mathbb{Z}S_{n}L(m)\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}L(m)
are defined by applying the following
Skein relation

[
BL93
]
to each double point

(4.3)

\includegraphics[width=25.0pt,valign={c}]{Ressources/double_point_skein_relation}\longmapsto\includegraphics[width=25.0pt,valign={c}]{Ressources/double_point_skein_relation_+}-\includegraphics[width=25.0pt,valign={c}]{Ressources/double_point_skein_relation_-},

where it is understood that the terms are identical outside of the part shown. Thus, for
\gamma\in S_{n}L(m)
a singular string link with
n
double points, its image
\del_{n}(\gamma)\in\mathbb{Z}L(m)
is an alternating sum of all possible resolutions of the
n
double points.

A string link invariant
V\colon L(m)\rightarrow A
valued in an abelian group
A
naturally extends to a
Z
-linear map
V\colon\mathbb{Z}L(m)\rightarrow A
. We say that
V
is a
Vassiliev invariant
or
finite type invariant
of degree
n
if it vanishes on
\del_{n+1}(\mathbb{Z}S_{n+1}L(m))
. In particular, a Vassiliev invariant of degree
n
induces a functional on
\mathcal{A}_{n}L(m)
. Two string links
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in L(m)
are
V_{n}
-equivalent
if they cannot be distinguished by Vassiliev invariants of degree
<n
or, equivalently, if
\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}
belongs to
\del_{n}(\mathbb{Z}S_{n}L(m))
.

There is a realization map
R^{v}\colon\mathcal{A}^{\FI}(m)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{A}L(m)
(the
v
refers to
Vassiliev
), which is a morphism of
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras. Originally,
R^{v}
was defined on the presentation of
\mathcal{A}^{\FI}(m)
by chord diagrams, by sending a chord diagram to a singular string link realizing it
[
Bar95
]
. However, one can define this realization map on Feynman (forest) diagrams using claspers
38
38

38

This defines the same realization map since degree
1
clasper surgeries are crossing changes

[
Hab00
, Section 6]
. This was made precise by Conant and Teichner in the proof of
[
CT04
, Theorem 1.1]
. Following their argument, we can construct a surjective
39
39

39

Surjectivity follows from Section 6 in
[
Hab00
]
, where Habiro defines the Vassiliev filtration using forest claspers instead of singular string links.
morphism of graded
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras

(4.4)

R^{v}\colon\mathcal{A}^{\FI}(m)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{A}L(m)

which in degree
n
sends a forest diagram
F\in\mathcal{D}_{n}(m)
to

[\gamma_{0};T_{1},\dots,T_{s}]\coloneqq\sum_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\subset\{T_{1},\dots,T_{s}\}}(-1)^{s-\lvert\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rvert}(\gamma_{0}^{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{A}_{n}L(m)

where
T_{1}\cup\dots\cup T_{s}
is a forest clasper realizing
F
,
\gamma_{0}
denotes the trivial string link on
m
strands and
\gamma^{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}_{0}
denotes the result of clasper surgery on
\gamma_{0}
along the trees contained in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
. That
R^{v}
is well-defined follows from
[
Hab00
, Section 6]
and
[
CT04
]
.

Remark 4.1.1.1
.

A forest clasper realizing a forest diagram
F
is constructed as in the proof of Proposition
3.1.4.1
, but with multiple trees at the same time and with an additional positive half-twist near a chosen leaf of each tree. Those half-twits are important in order for
\STU
relations to be realized with correct signs. This explains the footnote on page 68 in
[
Hab00
]
.
∎

Over
\mathbb{Q}
, the realization map
R^{v}\colon\mathcal{A}^{\FI}(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{A}L(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}
is an isomorphism, with inverse induced by the Kontsevich integral
[
Kon93
,
Bar95
]
.

4.1.2.
The Goussarov–Habiro conjecture

From Habiro’s definition of the Vassiliev filtration using claspers
40
40

40

And from Goussarov’s work as well.
, it follows that
C_{n}
-equivalence implies
V_{n}
-equivalence for string links in
L(m)
. Goussarov and Habiro independently conjectured the converse:

Conjecture

(Goussarov–Habiro conjecture for string links in the cylinder)
.

For all
m\geq 1
and
n\geq 1
, any
V_{n}
-equivalent string links on
m
strands are
C_{n}
-equivalent.

Since
C_{n}
-equivalence implies
V_{n}
-equivalence, any
C_{n}
-trivial string link
\gamma\in L_{n}(m)
is automatically
V_{n}
-equivalent to the trivial string link
1
, hence
\gamma-1
belongs to
\del_{n}(\mathbb{Z}S_{n}L(m))
. This yields the
comparison map

[
Hab00
, Section 8.2]

\chi\colon\mathcal{L}L(m)\rightarrow\mathcal{A}L(m)\colon[\gamma]\mapsto[\gamma-1],

which is injective if and only if the Goussarov–Habiro conjecture is true. In particular, it is injective when
m=1
by the Goussarov–Habiro theorem for knots
[
Hab00
, Theorem 6.18]
.

Exploiting the
dimension subgroup property
in characteristic zero, Massuyeau showed the following rational version of the Goussarov–Habiro conjecture:

Theorem 4.1.2.1

(Massuyeau
[
Mas07
]
)
.

Over
\mathbb{Q}
, the map
\chi\colon\mathcal{L}L(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}L(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}
is injective.

The realization maps
R,R^{v}
and the comparison map
\chi
combine into the following commutative square of graded (Lie or Hopf)
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras:

(4.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{L}^{\FI}(m)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Prim(\mathcal{A}^{\FI}(m))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}^{\FI}(m)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{L}L(m)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}L(m)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{v}}

When tensoring everything with
\mathbb{Q}
, we obtain:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{L}^{\FI}(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Prim(\mathcal{A}^{\FI}(m)_{\mathbb{Q}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}^{\FI}(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{L}L(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}L(m)_{\mathbb{Q}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{GH}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{v}}

where
Z^{v}
is the inverse to
R^{v}
by Kontsevich’s theorem,
\iota
is an isomorphism by Theorem
2.4.5.1
, the left triangle commutes and
R
becomes an isomorphism by Theorem
3.2.2.1
. Therefore,
\chi
is injective, which gives an alternative proof the rational Goussarov–Habiro conjecture.
